# Verified configs

<!-- Only the text between the verified-configs begin and end markers is generated; a re-render replaces it and
keeps everything else in this file as it is. -->

This file lists every model config in the installed MMDetection and MMSegmentation model zoos, one row each, with
the check done for it: whether this library's split model computes exactly what the stock (unsplit) OpenMMLab model
computes. It is a check of equality, not of accuracy: no mAP or mIoU is measured. The same table, with every column,
is in [verified_configs.csv](verified_configs.csv).

## Status

- **SUPPORTED — full verification**: checked with the released weights loaded; every result is identical to stock,
  or differs only in a minor way that was measured and explained and is not caused by the split (the status then
  ends in *(minor differences)* and the cell's note says why).
- **SUPPORTED — Verified - Config only (valid checkpoint unavailable)**: every result is identical with the config's
  model and seeded random weights; there is no usable released checkpoint ("what works / what does not" says why).
- **SUPPORTED — partially verified**: everything that was checked is identical, but some checks could not be run.
- **PARTIALLY SUPPORTED**: some part does not work on the split model, or is broken in stock itself; "what works /
  what does not" says which. The sub-label (*fully verified*, *Verified - Config only (valid checkpoint unavailable)* or *partially verified*)
  says how the working part was checked, as above.
- **UNSUPPORTED**: the library cannot split this config, the config does not load here, or stock itself fails on
  it; the reason is given. A model that is broken in stock is never counted as working, even when the split model
  fails the same way.
- **NOT VERIFIED**: the library accepts the config, but the check could not run it here; the reason is given.


**Training**: supported with limitations. For every model that runs in training mode here, one forward and
  backward pass was verified identical to stock (the loss, every buffer and every parameter's gradient); full
  training schedules run by mmengine's `Runner` are untested.

The rest of this file is the detail.

## How each config was checked

The split model and the stock model are built from the same config, with the same released weights, or with the
same seeded random weights where no released checkpoint could be used, and compared at cut 0, cut 1 and the last cut
on the same batch of real images (COCO val2017 for detection, ADE20K validation for segmentation): the training
loss, the per-image loss, the raw head outputs, the predictions on the config's own test pipeline at its own score
thresholds, and one training step's buffers and gradients. "Equal" means bit-identical (`torch.equal`). Everything
ran with deterministic algorithms on CPU; a GPU (deterministic, TF32 off) was used only where an mmcv op of the model
has no CPU kernel.

The middle cuts were spot-checked too: 21 registered models (detection and segmentation, ResNet and Swin, including
GLIP, GroundingDINO, DDQ-DETR and Cascade R-CNN) at cuts 2 and 3 with their released weights, on the same comparisons.
All were equal, with the same exceptions as at the other cuts: one per-image loss `close` (FCOS at cut 3), and raw
output not available for GLIP, GroundingDINO, MaskFormer and Mask2Former.

## What the columns mean

| column | meaning |
|---|---|
| config | the config file under the package's `configs/` folder, without `.py` |
| model type, backbone | the config's `model.type` and `model.backbone.type` |
| released weights | whether OpenMMLab released a checkpoint for this config |
| registered name | the name this library registers for the config, if any (`build_split_model('<name>')`) |
| status | see "Status" above |
| what works / what does not | for PARTIALLY SUPPORTED, what works and what does not; otherwise what was not run, or why the released checkpoint could not be used |
| released: load | the split model built by this library with the released checkpoint holds exactly the weights stock holds after OpenMMLab loads it |
| released: … | the comparisons below, with the released checkpoint loaded on both sides |
| random: … | the same comparisons with the same seeded random weights on both sides. Random-weight inference used score thresholds of 0 so that something is predicted; it is a weaker check and does not count towards full verification |
| loss | the batched training loss, every term, with every normalisation layer frozen (`freeze_norms=True`) and the backbone in eval mode |
| per-image | `backend(mode='loss', individual_losses=True)`: each image's loss, compared with stock's loss on that image alone within `torch.allclose`'s default tolerances (stock's own loss changes by about 1e-6 between batch sizes). Per-image losses are a feature for training with a different loss weight per image; stock has no such mode, so they are not a stock equivalent. The frontend runs once on the batch and the backend per image, so with unfrozen norms the frontend's normalisation statistics come from the whole batch. Users who need exact per-image behaviour including norms should run `feature_frontend` per image too. |
| raw | the raw head outputs (stock's `forward(mode='tensor')`) |
| inference | the predictions (boxes, scores, labels, masks, segmentation maps) on the config's test pipeline at its own score thresholds; when neither side predicts anything on the first two images, more images are used |
| training: loss, buffers, grads | training mode with the config's own normalisation settings (nothing frozen), random weights: the batched loss, every buffer after the forward pass, and every parameter's gradient after one backward pass. The CSV also has the raw output in training mode and the buffers after it |
| cuts | the cut points compared: 0, 1 and the last one (the number of backbone stages) |
| device | CPU, or the GPU that ran the comparison |
| tested as | configs that define the same model (the same `model` settings apart from `init_cfg`, `pretrained` and `test_cfg`) share their random-weight results; this names the config that was run. A config whose `test_cfg` differs does not inherit the raw and inference results. Released-weight results are always the config's own |
| notes | why a config is not supported, and other facts about it |

What a cell says (the CSV has the detailed value of every cell):

| cell | meaning |
|---|---|
| equal | identical (`torch.equal`) at every cut listed; when the cuts differ, each value lists its cuts in brackets |
| close | the per-image loss agrees within `torch.allclose`'s default tolerances |
| equal\* | identical apart from an effect of stock itself (its own nondeterminism / the batch-size effect on per-image losses / a released checkpoint that leaves keys unloaded in stock too); the note says which |
| broken in stock | stock itself fails here (it does not build / a checkpoint does not load / it predicts nothing / both sides raise the same error); not counted as working |
| not supported | the split model does not do this here (at this cut / this output, such as raw where stock's own tensor mode is broken / slide-mode inference (`test_cfg.mode='slide'`) at a cut above 0) |
| not checked | the check could not run this (missing data or packages / no usable checkpoint); neither a pass nor a failure |
| differs | a real difference between the split model and stock |
| — | not run |

Every cell other than equal, close and — carries a note number [n]; the notes are under the tables.

## Notes

1. Needs scikit-image, not installed in the verification environment.
2. An mmcv op of this model has no CPU kernel.
3. It needs precomputed proposal files, which the check did not have.
4. The config does not build with the installed MMDetection (its CascadeRPNHead needs num_classes).
5. The unsplit model's own prediction step fails on this batch with every threshold at 0.
6. OpenMMLab's own forward(mode='tensor') is broken for this model family.
7. MMDetection's GLIP language fusion does not work with the installed transformers version.
8. OpenMMLab's own forward(mode='tensor') is broken for this model family; this library raises an explained error instead.
9. Training-mode equality shown with drop-path off (verification only): with the config's own drop-path the split model draws the random drop-path and dropout masks in a different order than the stock model, so the two are not bit-identical; with drop-path off the loss, every gradient and every buffer are identical.
10. The config sets no box/class losses and the defaults do not fit its head.
11. An inference-only config (its head has no training assigner), so there is no loss.
12. Training-mode equality shown with drop-path off, and with language features computed first (verification only): with drop-path on, computing the language features before the backbone (as the stock model does) makes the loss, every gradient and every buffer identical, and so does turning drop-path off.
13. Compared one image at a time: mmcv's masked_conv2d takes batch size 1 in eval mode.
14. It needs semantic segmentation maps (such as COCO-Stuff), which the check did not have.
15. Raw and inference verified at all cuts; loss only at cut 0 (its loss runs a teacher network on the raw image, which a split model does not have at cut > 0).
16. It reads a teacher config by a path relative to the MMDetection source tree, which an installed MMDetection does not have.
17. A video config (YouTube-VIS), whose data the check did not have.
18. Per-image loss: the split model runs the frontend on the whole batch of 2 images, as it does for every model, and the stock ResNeSt backbone's output depends slightly on the batch size (stock alone, a batch of 2 against one image, differs at every stage), so the per-image losses differ slightly.
19. On the GPU the stock model's own backward pass is not deterministic with mmcv's synchronised BatchNorm: two runs of the stock model differ by up to 3e-7 in the gradients; the split model's gradients are within that, and its loss and buffers are identical.
20. Not checked in this run: TOOD's loss reads the training epoch, which only mmdet's training loop sets, and this run did not set it (the released-weight runs did).
21. The unsplit model's own seeded random initialisation gives non-finite weights.
22. It needs a module from an MMDetection project that is not installed.
23. Slide-mode inference (test_cfg.mode='slide') is not supported by the split model at a cut above 0 (a planned feature); cut 0 works.
24. This library refuses raw output for this model family before running it, while the unsplit model runs its backbone in training mode before it fails, so their normalisation statistics cannot match.
25. It needs GDAL (change-detection data), which is not installed.
26. The config's data preprocessor sets both size and size_divisor, which the stock model rejects.
27. The unsplit model predicts nothing with its released checkpoint: the config's pytorch-style preprocessing does not match the caffe-trained checkpoint (an upstream config problem); the split matches it.
28. One run differed (max abs 5.1e-06, max rel 2.6e-05) and did not repeat when rerun; the rerun is shown.
29. Per-image loss: the split model runs the frontend on the whole batch of 2 images, as it does for every model; with trained GC blocks the stock backbone's output depends slightly on the batch size (about 1e-5), which changes one image's proposals and so the random state the next image's proposal sampler starts from. The split computes exactly the stock model on batch-of-2 features.
30. The released checkpoint leaves 18 model key(s) unloaded in the unsplit model too (roi_head); the split loads it the same way.
31. The released checkpoint leaves 1 model key(s) unloaded in the unsplit model too (dn_query_generator); the split loads it the same way.
32. The stock Libra R-CNN is not reproducible from run to run: mmdet's positive sampler draws from a Python set of tensors, whose order changes between runs. With that order fixed on both sides (verification only), the split model is bit-identical at every cut (loss, per-image loss, raw output and predictions).
33. The released checkpoint was not available to the check.
34. Loss verified in both training phases (epoch 0 and 4 set in mmengine's message hub, as mmdet's training loop sets it).
35. TridentNet's backbone, in training mode, stacks its three branches along the batch dimension; when that stage is in the frontend (the last cut), the per-image backend takes one branch per image instead of three, so the per-image loss is not available at the last cut (the batched loss is identical).
36. The released checkpoint leaves 2 model key(s) unloaded in the unsplit model too (mask_head); the split loads it the same way.
37. Not inherited: this config's test_cfg differs from the tested config's.
<!-- verified-configs:end tables -->

## Environment

<!-- verified-configs:begin environment -->
torch 2.6.0+cu124, mmcv 2.1.0, mmengine 0.10.7, mmdet 3.3.0, mmseg 1.2.2, transformers 5.1.0. The runs used two machines with identical versions of these packages. GPU runs: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2080 Ti (CUDA 12.4) and NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3080 Ti (CUDA 12.4), with deterministic algorithms and TF32 off; every other run on CPU with deterministic algorithms. Runs from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-12.
<!-- verified-configs:end environment -->
